## Legend

- **Status**: `OPEN` | `SPEC DONE` | `IN PROGRESS` | `DONE` | `BLOCKED`

## 1. Security — CRITICAL (P0)

These block real money. No exceptions. Source: SECURITY-AUDIT.md

| # | Finding | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|-------|--------|
| C1 | Withdrawal race condition (double-withdrawal exploit) — add per-wallet async mutex | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A1) — deployed 86c77f2 |
| C2 | No viem nonce management — nonce collision on concurrent TXs | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A0) — AUDIT-13 proof: T-A0-EDGE-004 |
| C3 | Non-atomic dual write on unlock — crash between learnings/earnings writes | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A2) — deployed 86c77f2 |
| C4 | Stuck settlement infinite loop — no max-retry, no auto-refund | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A2) — deployed 86c77f2 |
| C5 | x402 payment validation entirely external — no local crypto verification | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A4) — deployed 86c77f2 |
| C6 | Private key plaintext on disk — move to env var minimum | BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A0) — T-A0-UNIT-001 + S3 grep |
| C7 | Challenge nonce reuse (replay attack) — consume before verify | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A3) |
| C8 | Pending balance deducted before broadcast — use reservation model | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A1) — deployed 86c77f2 |

**Review**: GOV-3 (Security Auditor) + SPEC-1 (Crypto Auditor) sign off on every fix. Separate context from Builder.

## 1b. Security — Sensitivity Filter (SPEC-SF, P0)

GOV-3 audit found 2 CRITICAL, 3 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 4 LOW in the learning pipeline. Prerequisite to Build-2 A-series.

| # | Finding | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|-------|--------|
| SF-C1 | 6 critical pattern gaps (SSH, Slack, Stripe, Google, npm, PEM) | BUILD-2 | DONE — patterns added to `lib/sensitivity-filter.js` |
| SF-C2 | sk_live_ not caught by generic sk- prefix | BUILD-2 | DONE — dedicated `stripe_key` pattern |
| SF-H2 | redactMatch leaks too many chars (6+4=10) | BUILD-2 | DONE — changed to 3+2=5 chars |
| SF-H3 | No unlock_price ceiling — DoS vector | BUILD-2 | DONE — `MAX_UNLOCK_PRICE = 1.00` enforced |
| SF-M1 | scanLearning() not wrapped in try/catch — fail open | BUILD-2 | DONE — fail closed (returns 500) |
| SF-M2 | PATTERNS missing /g flag invariant assertion | BUILD-2 | DONE — assertion at module load |
| SF-M3 | aws_secret matches substrings of larger base64 strings | BUILD-2 | DONE — word-boundary lookahead/lookbehind |
| SF-M4 | password pattern false-positives on null/undefined/placeholders | BUILD-2 | DONE — negative lookahead |
| SF-M5 | Dedup re-hashes every existing learning on every POST | BUILD-2 | DONE — pre-computed `body_hash` on new learnings; legacy fallback |
| SF-M6 | 409 Conflict exposes existing_id + existing_title | BUILD-2 | DONE — stripped from response |
| SF-L3 | Docs out of sync with live patterns | BUILD-2 | DONE — guide, punch-list, tasks updated |

**Tests**: 46 new cases (T-SF-UNIT-001/018, T-SF-INT-001/012, T-SF-EDGE-001/016). All 72 tests pass.

**GOV-3 Sign-off required** before Build-2 executes A-series (A3→A0→A2→A1→A4).

## 2. Security — HIGH (P1)

Block production traffic. Source: SECURITY-AUDIT.md

| # | Finding | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|-------|--------|
| H1 | No EIP-712 structured signing — implement typed data with domain separator | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A3) |
| H2 | Timestamp reuse on withdrawal signatures — add server-generated nonce | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A3) |
| H3 | No rate limit on /wallet/challenge — max 5 per wallet per 10 min | BUILD-2 | VERIFIED (A3) |
| H4 | Facilitator DDoS = revenue disabled — local verification fallback | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE (A4) — deployed 86c77f2 |
| H5 | Admin token — no expiry, no rotation, no scope limiting | BUILD-2 | DONE (A4) — deployed 86c77f2 |

## 3. Security — MEDIUM (P2)

Fix before scale. Source: SECURITY-AUDIT.md

| # | Finding | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|-------|--------|
| M-A | In-memory rate limit lost on restart | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, persist to disk on write + restore on boot |
| M-B | Earnings migration sets pending_balance without validating against learnings | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, cross-check validation on earnings load |
| M-C | Settlement JSONL append-only, never compacted | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, compaction at threshold with atomic rewrite |
| M-D | Backup cleanup runs on every safeWrite() call | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, throttled to once/hour max |
| M-E | Silent JSON parse failures at startup | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, loadDataFile with critical vs non-critical distinction |
| M-F | No private key rotation mechanism | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, /admin/stage-key endpoint |
| M-G | Concurrent backup cleanup race condition | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 08, cleanupRunning mutex with finally block |
| M-H | Ghost settlement on-chain check — `resolveStuckSettlements` should verify on-chain like `resolveProcessingSettlements` | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 01 Track 1 |
| M-I | Daemon overlap guard — `let running = false` flag to prevent concurrent daemon ticks | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 01 Track 1 |
| M-J | Reservation orphan cross-reference — filter against pending/retry settlements before releasing reservations | BUILD-2 | DONE — Sprint 01 Track 1 |

## Execution Sequence

```
Phase 0 (parallel tracks):

  Track A: Security Fixes
  C1-C8 → Review (GOV-3 + SPEC-1) → H1-H5 → Review → Deploy → Live QA

  Track B: Payment Infrastructure
  0.1 Account System → 0.2 Dual Auth → 0.3 Credits → 0.4 Stripe → 0.5 Earnings
  (GOV-2 reviews Stripe terms in parallel with 0.1-0.3)

  Track C: Autonomous Publishing (can start during Track B)
  1.1 System Prompt Template → 1.2 Learning Extractor → 1.3 OpenClaw Adapter

  Track D: Brand (runs parallel, blocked items unblock as product ships)
  B-1 Brand Guidelines → B-2 through B-7 (assets) → B-8/B-9/B-10 (copy, post-product)

Gate: All P0 items DONE + all review sign-offs → Launch
```

| # | Task | Owner | Status |
|---|------|-------|--------|
| NF-1 | Multi-environment onboarding — per-LLM setup instructions for builders with multiple agents | SPEC-3 + BUILD-2 | DONE — 5 templates in `prompts/` (W1-D1, verified 2026-03-30) |

| NF-3 | `npx auxilo-mcp init` / `add-environment` CLI — builder onboarding tool | BUILD-2 | ✅ **BUILT 2026-07-19 (Wave 3.4, `wave34-scoped-keys`)** — `npx auxilo init` (+ `auxilo-mcp init` delegation): zero-prompt scoped-key minting for CI / second machines via the device flow (`--scope read\|earnings-read\|contribute` default contribute, `--label` default init-\<hostname\>, `--env-file` 0600 idempotent writer, `--save` opt-in credentials write, `--json`); warns on pre-3.4-server scope downgrade (status echo). Spec `~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-WAVE34-2026-07-19.md`. CLI half is POST-0.9.3 (ship as npm **0.9.4** — no version bumped yet); server half (device-flow scope/label/cap/echo) rides the next deploy and degrades gracefully until then. NOT merged/deployed. |
| NF-4 | Scoped API keys per environment — one account, multiple keys with labels | BUILD-1 → BUILD-2 | DONE — multi-key with labels, 3 endpoints (W1-D2, verified 2026-03-30). **D2 TIGHTENED 2026-07-19 (Wave 3.4)**: taxonomy now read < **earnings-read** < contribute < admin (single-sourced lib/accounts.js; `GET /account/earnings` moved read→earnings-read); **admin never issuable** via API or device flow (403 ADMIN_SCOPE_NOT_ISSUABLE); pre-D2 keys grandfathered via `effective_scope` (legacy read ⇒ earnings-read = exact prior capability set — existing keys keep working); **live bug fixed**: dualAuthDynamic's exact-match scope check 403'd the default contribute setup key on the API-key/credit unlock path (now rank-based) — **regressed 3d365e1 / 2026-04-18 (P2.1a)**: exact-match scope check + 'read' requiredScope + contribute device keys landed in the same commit; API-key credit unlocks dead on prod until this wave (Gate-A reviewer CONFIRMED live by booting both servers); rotation added (`POST /account/api-keys/rotate`, capability-preserving, session-by-label or key-SELF-only) + key-caller self-revoke + session-or-key listing; full route→scope audit table in spec §3. The lost `specs/WAVE1-BUILD-D2-SCOPED-KEYS.md` is superseded by `~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-WAVE34-2026-07-19.md` (design of record). NOT merged/deployed. |
| D2-F2 | **Key-store growth + rotation abuse (Wave 3.4 Gate-A reviewer F2)** — (a) the labeled-creation 10-key cap counts INACTIVE entries (`account.api_keys.length`) while rotation permanently appends dead deactivated entries → 10 rotations bricks labeled minting for the account; (b) `POST /account/api-keys/rotate` has no cap and no rate limit → a stolen key looping rotate grows accounts.json without bound (DoS amplifier — the file is parsed on every authenticated request). Fix direction: count ACTIVE keys only at the labeled cap (device-authorize path already does), compact-or-cap rotated-out entries (e.g. keep last N per label or prune inactive beyond a ceiling), rate-limit rotate (per-account, reuse the AU-8 limiter idiom). | P1 fast-follow | GOV-3 + BUILD-1 | ✅ **BUILT 2026-07-19 (Wave-5B, `wave5b-review-closures`; spec `~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-WAVE5B-2026-07-19.md` §3)** — (a) labeled cap now counts ACTIVE keys only AND the fully-uncapped legacy name-path gained the same cap; (b) `compactRotatedKeys` (lib/accounts.js): rotation keeps the newest 3 rotated-out entries per label, revocation audit rows untouched, replacement entry stays LAST (caller index math holds), rotate route calls `rebuildKeyIndex()` whenever compaction shifted positions (stale `key_index` would stamp last_used_at on the wrong entry); (c) `POST /account/api-keys/rotate` per-account limit 10/hour (in-memory sliding window, isLearnRateLimited idiom; deliberately NOT in the M-A persisted payload — compaction is the durable control, restart-reset acceptable). 12-rotation loop test pins total store size ≤ 1 live + 3 debris. Tests in `test/wave5b-review-closures.test.js`. **Gate-A F5:** the create→revoke loop still grew the store (revocation debris was permanent and LABELS are attacker-chosen, so per-label ceilings bound nothing) — `capInactiveDebris`: GLOBAL newest-15 ceiling on ALL inactive entries per account (label-agnostic; chosen over a creation rate limit, which only slows the same unbounded growth), revoke route stamps `revoked_at` + caps + `rebuildKeyIndex()` on shift; rotation path applies both ceilings. Hard bound: ≤10 active + 15 dead per account. 30-unique-label churn test + mutation-verified. **Gate-A F6 (adjacent, same commit):** /learn's own sensitivity call had no mutation pin (stubbing the classifier left the whole suite green) — loud pins added on the /learn slice: classifier invoked, fail-closed fallback, verdict gates hold reason AND seamless predicate, flagged verdict persisted; reviewer's stub mutation now kills. |
| NF-5 | Dynamic pricing engine activation — `lib/pricing.js` exists but not wired to live pricing | BUILD-2 | DONE — pricing wired to 3 routes + cron + 2 analytics endpoints (W1-E1/E2, verified 2026-03-30) |

## 15. Governance: Documentation Rules

**These rules apply to ALL future work. No exceptions.**

### Rule 1: No Undocumented Features
Every new feature, endpoint, pricing change, policy change, or UX flow MUST be reflected in its domain's source-of-truth document BEFORE the PR/deploy is considered complete. The build spec must list which docs to update.

### Rule 2: INDEX.md Is the Map
`docs/INDEX.md` maps every document to its domain and declares source-of-truth status. New documents MUST be registered in INDEX.md. If a document is superseded, it gets a banner AND an INDEX.md update.

### Rule 3: One Source of Truth Per Domain
Each domain (product, marketplace, pricing, GTM, etc.) has exactly ONE source-of-truth document as designated in INDEX.md. Business decisions live in domain docs, NOT in MEMORY.md. MEMORY.md stores operational context only (credentials, VM IDs, session state).

### Rule 4: Build Specs Reference Docs
Every build spec (`specs/WAVE1-*.md`, `specs/BUILD-SPEC-*.md`) must include a "Documentation Impact" section listing which docs need updating. Reviewers check this before sign-off.

### Rule 5: Stale Detection
Any document with "Last updated" older than 30 days gets flagged in the next sprint planning. The sprint must include a documentation review item.

### Rule 6: Punch List Tracks Everything
Every planned enhancement, optimization, or addon gets a line item in PUNCH-LIST.md with: task description, owner, status, and spec file reference (if applicable). Nothing lives only in conversation history or memory bullets.

---

## 16. Review & Inspection Gates

**Reviews are gate-based, not calendar-based. Every change triggers the relevant gates before merge/deploy. No exceptions.**

### Gate A: Every Deploy/Merge (mandatory)

| Gate | Owner | Pass Criteria |
|------|-------|---------------|
| **Engineering Review** | BUILD-1 (Architect) | Spec matches architecture, no unintended side effects, fallback chains intact |
| **Code Inspection** | BUILD-4 (QA) | `node --check server.js` passes, all tests green, no regressions, scope check (only listed files modified) |
| **Security Review** | GOV-3 (Security Auditor) | Auth correct, input validated, no secrets in code, OWASP top 10 checked, no new attack surface |
| **Documentation Review** | GOV-1 (PM) | §15 Rule 1 enforced — domain docs updated, INDEX.md current, PUNCH-LIST.md updated |

### Gate B: Scope-Triggered (only when change touches that domain)

| Gate | Trigger | Owner | Pass Criteria |
|------|---------|-------|---------------|
| **Financial Integrity** | Any file in outputs/, financial model, investor deck, pricing assumptions, revenue projections | GOV-4 (CFO) | Every number traces to FOUNDATION.md, FOUNDATION.lock hash valid, no stale assumptions, cross-file sync verified |
| **Crypto/Payment** | Wallets, x402, pricing, payouts | SPEC-1 (Crypto Auditor) | Payment dedup intact, price bounds enforced, no fund loss paths |
| **Agent UX** | API responses, MCP tools, discovery, search | SPEC-2 (Agent UX) | Backward-compatible, agent can discover/query/unlock without breaking, error messages actionable |
| **Builder UX** | Onboarding, earnings, dashboard, API keys | SPEC-3 (Builder UX) | Flow completable end-to-end, earnings visible, no dead ends |
| **Legal/Compliance** | Terms, privacy, data handling, public claims, payments | GOV-2 (Compliance) | ToS/Privacy consistent, GENIUS Act compliant, no unapproved data collection |
| **Brand/Narrative** | Copy, messaging, external-facing text, landing page | BUILD-3 (Brand) | Voice consistent with BRAND_GUIDELINES.md, no jargon drift, ICP messaging intact |
| **SEO** | Page content, meta tags, URLs, sitemap | BUILD-3 (Brand) | Title/description tags present, structured data valid, canonical URLs, no broken links |
| **GEO (Generative Engine Optimization)** | llms.txt, agent.json, MCP descriptions, OpenAPI spec, instructions field | SPEC-2 (Agent UX) | Agent-discoverable, tool descriptions accurate, OpenAPI paths match live routes, llms.txt current |

### Gate C: Milestone Deep Dives (at major releases or when entering operate mode)

| Review | Trigger | Owner | Pass Criteria |
|--------|---------|-------|---------------|
| **Competitive** | Major release or quarterly | GOV-1 (PM) | Landscape scanned, moat intact, pricing competitive, no new threats unaddressed |
| **Catalog Health** | Major release or quarterly | GOV-1 (PM) | No stale learnings (>90d unrated), pricing distribution healthy, category gaps identified |
| **Infrastructure/Dependency** | Major release or quarterly | BUILD-4 (QA) | `npm audit` clean, prod VM healthy, backups verified, uptime >99% |
| **Financial** | Major release or quarterly | GOV-2 (Compliance) | Revenue tracking accurate, payout math verified, tax reporting current |
| **Full UX Audit** | Major release | SPEC-2 + SPEC-3 | All user journeys tested, mobile responsive, accessibility basics met |

### How It Works in Practice

1. Build spec lists files to modify → that determines which Gate B reviews activate
2. Builder (BUILD-2) implements the spec
3. Gate A reviews run (all four, every time)
4. Gate B reviews run (only the ones triggered by scope)
5. All reviewers sign "SHIP" or "SHIP WITH FIXES" → fixes applied → re-review
6. GOV-1 (PM) gives final sign-off
7. Deploy to the prod VM
8. Post-deploy smoke test (BUILD-4)

**The agent who builds is never the only agent who reviews.**

### 18b. Wave-3 client Gate-A reviewer findings (N-series, 2026-07-19) — closed by Wave 5C

Source: wave-3 client Gate-A adversarial review (`wave3-client-funnel`, merged f6282fd). Rows recorded here at closure — spec/design of record: `~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-WAVE5C-2026-07-19.md` (branch `wave5c-client-closures`).

| # | Finding | Priority | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|----------|-------|--------|
| N1 | **Adapter read-size cap missing** — `discoverSessions()` returns `bytes` per the §4.1 contract but `readSession()` read unbounded (`fs.readFileSync`; 54MB proven in the wild) — a multi-GB transcript OOMs a constrained sweeper node, all to feed a pipeline that truncates to 30k chars. | P1 | BUILD-2 | ✅ **BUILT 2026-07-19** (`wave5c-client-closures`, npm-0.9.4 rider). Cap lives on the BASE adapter path — concrete `TranscriptSource.readSessionCapped()` (scripts/sources/source.interface.js): fresh `statSync` size (else `sessionRef.bytes`), over-cap throws `SessionTooLargeError` (`code SESSION_TOO_LARGE`) BEFORE any byte is read; ALL adapters incl. generic-jsonl fallback + future UC-3 self-registered ones inherit it (no per-adapter copies; no-shadowing + no-bare-`readSession`-call-site closure guards test-pinned). Runner (both call sites: sweep + `--transcript`): oversize = counted SKIP with stderr line (`oversize_skipped=N`) + ledger-marked `'oversize'` (probe-refused idiom), never a failure; single-file mode short-circuits BEFORE the raw readFileSync fallback (which would have been the same OOM). **Cap = 64MB default** (54MB proven-in-the-wild extracted successfully — a lower cap silently regresses real coverage; 64MB bounds worst-case RSS at low-hundreds-MB, multi-GB structurally refused), env-tunable `AUXILO_MAX_SESSION_BYTES` (positive int; garbage → default). Garbage-fixture + sweep-e2e + env-resolution + closure-guard tests: `test/wave5c-client-closures.test.js` N1-1…N1-5. |
| N3 | **Consent-gate had NO executable coverage** — reviewer proved flipping `extractionArmed = false → true` (bin/auxilo-cli.js cmdSetup ~:231) AND deleting the UC-1a else-cleanup branch each shipped silently: prior coverage was a string-position check + removal-helper unit tests; nothing executed the branch. Consent is the legal spine (UC-1a/GOV-3; agent-cannot-enroll doctrine). | P0 | BUILD-2 | ✅ **BUILT 2026-07-19** (`wave5c-client-closures`). Integration test drives the REAL CLI as a subprocess (`test/wave5c-client-closures.test.js` N3): fixture HOME (claude-code + cursor detected, pre-authed creds), scripted PROMPT DRIVER (answers written only when each prompt appears — up-front piped answers get dropped by readline, documented in the harness), local HTTP server playing `POST /extract/consent` in three modes: **(a)** 200 → sentinel + SessionEnd hook + cursor shim exist, exactly 1 grant call with the right key; **(b)** 500 → loud stderr, NOT armed, zero capture artifacts, pre-seeded STALE hook/shim artifacts REMOVED; **(c)** decline → ZERO consent calls + same cleanup asserts. MCP registration + SessionStart notice pinned consent-independent in all three. **Mutation-verified (apply→fail→revert→prove): the `extractionArmed` flip kills (b)+(c) (10 tests: 7 pass/2 fail, exit 1); deleting the else-cleanup branch kills (b)+(c) (7/2, exit 1); reverted tree green (exit 0).** |
| N4 | **UC-5 spec pre-R2 gate items** — (a) `auxilo_accept_terms` was "Qualified yes" for remote (in-chat assent unresolved); (b) OAuth dynamic client registration had no abuse limits (anonymous store-growth DoS + phishing-client mint; D2-F2 class). | P1 | BUILD-2 + GOV-2/GOV-3 | ✅ **RESOLVED BY AMENDMENT 2026-07-19** — `~/.auxilo/handoffs/SPEC-UC5-REMOTE-MCP-2026-07-19.md` amended in place (banner + amendment record): **A1** `auxilo_accept_terms` FIRMLY link-out — an agent tool-call must never constitute clickwrap assent (SPEC3-C1 agent-cannot-enroll + GOV-3 'evidentiary not preventive' doctrine bind a fortiori remotely); tool returns terms status + dashboard accept URL only, R2 tool table must expose no assent-capable parameter; former open decision §8.1 CLOSED. **A2** R3 acceptance now requires DCR abuse limits (§6.7): per-IP + global rate limit, client-store cap + never-authorized TTL eviction, DCR records inert until a real account authorizes, strict redirect-URI validation. Both marked pre-R2 gate items resolved. |

JWT session fills ownership only when no valid key is presented. Side effect: API-key-only submissions (no wallet, no JWT) now pass the identity gate. Regression tests T-LW15-17/18. | LW-16 | **Sensitivity-gated seamless publishing (two-layer)** — inverts the LW-13 "everything → pending_review" default. With moderation on, a fully-clean submission now publishes `approved` immediately (seamless); review is the exception triggered by a flag. **Layer 1 — regex** `lib/content-sensitivity.js` (`classifySensitivity`) catches confidential CONTENT the credential/injection/dedup screens miss — person/client/brand names, emails, private home paths, account/wallet ids, first-person proprietary context. **Measured regex recall on the 374 retracted-confidential learnings = 77.5% (misses 82** "proprietary-context phrased generically" items with no literal token). **Layer 2 — LLM** `lib/content-sensitivity-llm.js` (`classifySensitivityLLM`, `claude-haiku-4-5`, 6s hard timeout, fail-closed on timeout/error/missing-key) is a strict confidentiality classifier that catches the misses regex can't see. Combined as a **UNION** (`regex.sensitive \|\| llm.sensitive`) via `evaluateContentSensitivity` in server.js, awaited on /learn + /extract; **short-circuit** skips the LLM when regex flags, but regex-clean MUST consult the LLM before auto-approve. `sensitivity_source` (`regex`/`llm`/`both`) recorded alongside `sensitivity_signals`; both stripped from buyer projections. New `LLM_SENSITIVITY_ENABLED` toggle (default true; `false` → regex-only, NOT leak-safe). Curated allowlist keeps all 22 `outputs/SEED-VERIFIED.json` seeds seamless. /extract honors extractor verdict as a raise-only UNION. `FORCE_ALL_REVIEW` kill switch (opt-in, default false). `MODERATION_AUTO_APPROVE` retired (no-op). Recall harness `test/scripts/lw16-recall-eval.js` (refuses to fabricate; `--self-test` verifies wiring + 374/290/82 reconciliation without a key). Package 0.8.1 → 0.8.2. ## 21. Universal Client Support (UC) — every LLM / vibe-coding app / agent (2026-06-12)

Source: Tyler directive 2026-06-11 ("ensure our system works in every other LLM and vibe coding app, even agents like OpenClaw") + 3-agent research pass (repo baseline, IDE landscape, CLI/agent landscape — all web-verified June 2026). Spec: `specs/BUILD-SPEC-UNIVERSAL-CLIENTS.md` (capture-strategy classes H/G/P/R/T, client→class matrix, per-phase tests, consent requirements).

Key research findings: MCP is now universal (Linux Foundation; every surveyed client supports stdio MCP — registration is a config-writer problem). Claude Code's hook contract became the de-facto industry standard (Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, Cursor IDE+CLI, VS Code Copilot, Windsurf, Antigravity, Factory droid all ship session-end-class JSON-over-stdin hooks, several handing transcript_path directly) — our existing integration ports with shims, not rewrites. No transcript interchange standard exists (OTel GenAI = pre-stable telemetry schema; ACP = wire protocol, strategic later bet). Current reality: only Claude Code (hook) + OpenClaw (poll, no MCP) have extraction; Claude Desktop/Cursor are MCP-only; SUPPORTED-CLIENTS.md overpromises.

Hook into the refresh timer (server.js ~1873). Closes the continuing-obligation gap where a wallet linked clean is never re-checked. Trivial at current wallet count. | P1 | BUILD-2 + GOV-3 | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-20 — DEPLOYED** (main `c076021`). ## 25. Cold-Start Findings (2026-07-18)

| # | Finding | Priority | Owner | Status |
|---|---------|----------|-------|--------|
| CS-1 | **Fresh-install seeding TDZ crash** — the startup migration/seed blocks call `safeWrite()` during module init, but its backup-cleanup state (`let cleanupRunning` / `let lastBackupCleanup`) was declared ~120 lines *below* the seed block. On a cold start (empty `data/`), the seed write threw `Cannot access 'cleanupRunning' before initialization` inside the seed try/catch — mislabeled as "Failed to load seed knowledge", success log swallowed. The seed data landed on disk only because the TDZ read happens to sit after the atomic rename (one refactor away from real data loss), and the same TDZ sat under all four top-level migration write sites (AC-1/M-1/M-B), where a mid-block throw skips the paired earnings write — a partial-write hazard on populated installs. Prod never hit it (data dir populated); every fresh install did. | P1 | BUILD-2 | ✅ **FIXED 2026-07-18** — declarations hoisted next to the `BACKUP_DIR` setup, above every top-level `safeWrite()` call site. Verified by real cold boot against an empty temp data dir (pre-fix: TDZ error reproduced verbatim; post-fix: `Seeded 22 initial learnings` + backup cleanup runs at init). Regression: `test/cold-start-seed.test.js` — structural source-order guard (CI-enforced) + behavioral cold boot (self-skips where server runtime deps aren't installed). Negative control: both guards fail against the pre-fix code. Full suite 572 pass / 0 fail. |

| SPEC3-A3 | **Client review surfaces still render the pre-B1 model (drift vs live server)**: CLI `review` (`bin/auxilo-cli.js:527`) computes its own `clean` from `screens_passed` and prints "CLEAN (passed every screen)" + quality bands; dashboard "Select clean" (`public/dashboard.html`) filters `screens_passed` only. Neither consumes the live B1 `lane`/`why`/`approvable_count` contract, so the two surfaces disagree with each other AND with the server (observed live 2026-07-25: CLI "clean" = 20 items at q≥14, dashboard "clean" = 26, server `ready_to_publish` = 12 of 14). Includes the silent-skip UX defect: `--approve-clean` skipping unscored items is documented intent (`lib/self-review.js:312` — null quality skipped unless threshold explicitly 0) but the CLI never SAYS how many unscored items it excluded, so an operator reads "clean approved" as "everything safe approved". Fix: port both surfaces to the three lanes (`ready_to_publish`/`needs_score`/`needs_your_eyes` + per-row `why`), replace `--approve-clean`'s local selection with the server's lane verdict, and print excluded-count lines ("N unscored not included — review individually") on every bulk path. Also: `review --help` enters the interactive flow instead of printing flags, and the interactive `v` reads raw keypresses (TTY-only; piped stdin advances one item) — fold both into the same CLI pass. | P1 | BUILD-2 + SPEC-3 + SPEC-2 | **OPEN 2026-07-25** (found during live pending-queue review; B1 server contract live-verified same day, see SPEC3-B1 row). Confirmed remaining: pre-B1 rendering on both surfaces, client-side approve-clean selection, subcommand --help unhandled, hook-checker object-shape false-negative (reproduced live 2026-07-26; fix folded in — shared helper, both call sites). Design rulings: `--approve-ready` primary (`--approve-clean` hidden alias per B1 naming rule), selection = server lane verdict verbatim, `--min-quality <14` prints an explicit beyond-verdict warning, counted-confirmation rail untouched. **THE ACTIVE BUILD** (G1 queued behind). **Base rulings 2026-07-26: (1) STOP #2 (base not green) CONFIRMED bigger than reported — see CI-RED-0726; authorized base moved to `096c8da` post-fix. | BACKUP-RETENTION | **Off-box operator backups of the full prod store (`~/.auxilo/backups/auxilo-data-YYYYMMDD-090000.tgz`, daily at 09:00, 116 MB, 16 files) had NO retention rule — a deleted user's rows would persist there indefinitely, outside the served policy's 7-day on-box window.** Surfaced by the GOV2-DEL Phase 0 backup finding. **Tyler ruled 2026-09-05: "prune backups to 30 days."** One-off prune executed (the only file over 30 days was a 0-byte `…20260707….tgz.SUSPECT`, removed; oldest remaining 2026-08-24). Standing rule: retention-on-write in the daily backup job's own script (find -mtime +30 -delete after a successful write), so no second job is needed — pending location of the writer (not in ~/Library/LaunchAgents by name; see STATUS). Policy alignment: rider (d) covers on-box copies; the off-box rule keeps the operator side within 30 days, matching §4's "+30 days after deletion request" row. **STANDING RULE LANDED 2026-09-05:** the writer is `io.auxilo.backup` (launchd, daily 09:00) → `~/.auxilo/bin/auxilo-backup.sh`. It already rotated the newest 14 GOOD archives by count but never touched `.tgz.SUSPECT` files (one from 2026-08-16 survived) and a count bound alone lets the last 14 age forever if the job dies. Added an age bound after the rotation: `find $BK -maxdepth 1 -name 'auxilo-data-*' -mtime +30 -delete` (covers good and SUSPECT archives), header comment + OK line updated, `sh -n` clean, pre-edit copy kept as `auxilo-backup.sh.bak-2026-09-05`. Dry run today: nothing over 30 days. Effective off-box retention: 14 daily good copies, hard ceiling 30 days for anything. | P2 | GOV-2 + OPS-PM | DONE 2026-09-05 — one-off prune + standing 30-day age bound in the backup job. |

| SPEC3-E1b | **`account_vocab` generic-term false positives — the signal was suppressing its own queue.** Live 2026-08-06: pending 30, `by_signal.account_vocab`=21 (70% hold) against E1's own Phase-0 design gate of <=10%; `ready_to_publish` starved to 5 while approvable sat flat and the catalog held at 123 for 6 days. Every sampled flagged term was generic: ordinary English compounds (`already-vetted`, `race-condition`, `auto-generated`, `7-day`, `home-dir`, `image-processing`), ALL-CAPS emphasis (`VALUE`, `LOUDLY`), and public product names (`ChatGPT`, `LinkedIn`, `mcp-server`, `codex-desktop`). Root cause is STRUCTURAL, not a stale list: S1/S2 match any `-`/`_` token so ordinary compounds are indistinguishable from identifiers, and all three precision suppressors are inert in this corpus — cross-account contrast needs >=10 accounts (3 exist), approved-DF needs the term in >=3 approved learnings, and the wordlist holds only single words while compounds grow combinatorially. Fix makes the wordlist multiplicative: `isCompoundOfKnownTerms()` excludes a candidate when EVERY segment is known-or-numeric (one unknown segment keeps the flag — preserves `vandelay-brain`, `initech-pilot`, `acme_widget`; synthetic fixtures per GOV-3 identity ruling), new exclusion reason `static_common_dev_compound` keeps the two mechanisms separately auditable, wordlist +65 terms scoped to what the live queue actually flagged plus public product nouns. **Process note worth keeping:** a first pass added 186 speculative words including `private` and BROKE the ratified E1 fixture `private_queue` — internal identifiers are often two ordinary words, so generic identifier modifiers (private/public/shared/core/lane/tier/state/mode) silently disarm the signal. Trimmed to evidence-only; the wordlist header now carries that as a DO-NOT-ADD rule. Known accepted trade, documented at the call site: an all-ordinary-words compound whose MEANING is account-internal (`ready-to-publish`) now passes — the signal targets unknown proper nouns, never hard-blocks, and per-item screens still run. | P1 | BUILD-1 + CAT-1 + SPEC-3 | **BUILT 2026-08-06; GATE A COMPLETE 2026-08-27 — four reviews (BUILD-1, BUILD-4, GOV-3, GOV-1) all PASS-with-notes, zero blockers; reviewer-mandated remediation applied pre-merge and commits rebuilt (branch unpushed — history rewrite free): `shared`/`core`/`queue` removed from the base wordlist + `shared_queue` must-flag regression pin (convergent MED: the compound rule weaponized pre-existing DO-NOT-ADD-class entries), synthetic fixture swap (GOV-3 identity ruling), trade-example comment corrected to `auto-publish` (BUILD-1 — `ready-to-publish` still flags and was a false example), AGENT-LEARNING-GUIDE compound clause (GOV-1 merge-gating; CLOSES "docs/ E1 surface not yet reviewed" — all other E1 doc surfaces affirmed clean). Rebuilt code commit `57a4c08` (base a377ddb).** Verified post-remediation: 13/13 live false positives excluded, 0/9 real identifiers lost (7 pinned in the new file incl. `shared_queue`, plus `private_queue`, `ops-consumer` in the ratified E1 suite); full suite 1460 discovered / 0 fail; pin 1455->1460. **MERGED + DEPLOYED 2026-08-27: merge `2e0da6d`, CI green on the exact sha, Fly machine v65, boot clean. | EXT-0806 | **Extraction pipeline produced ZERO learnings for 3+ days and reported success the whole time.** Discovered by the 2026-08-06 supply cadence. Since `2026-08-03T21:52Z`, 30 consecutive runs log `local model not authenticated in this context (run \`claude\` and /login once); skipping deterministic extraction` and then return a normal result object — the caller prints `[runner] ✓ published=0 held=0 rejected=0 (extraction: client-skip)`. **A checkmark on a total no-op.** Capture/scrub/dedup/upload are all healthy; only the extraction step is skipped, which is why the catalog sat flat at 123 while the sweeper looked green. Two distinct problems. (a) AUTH — reproduced directly 2026-08-06: `claude -p` returns `Not logged in · Please run /login`, exit 0, BOTH inside and outside the tool sandbox, so it is a genuine credential failure and not an environment artifact. A `Claude Code-credentials` keychain item exists for the account but the CLI will not use it (expired/revoked; last real extraction `2026-08-02T14:51Z`). Critically, the Claude Code app session authenticates separately — so daily interactive use gives NO signal that the CLI login is dead, which is exactly why this ran silent for 3 days. Fix is Tyler-only (interactive `claude` + `/login`); an agent cannot authenticate. (b) SILENT-FAILURE DEFECT (ours, fixable) — `scripts/runner.js:392-394` returns a success-shaped `{published:0, held:0, rejected:0, extraction_id:'client-skip'}` on the skip path and the three caller log lines (`:923`, `:949`, `:1088`) stamp it with `✓`. Nothing counts consecutive skips, nothing alerts, and `~/.auxilo/extract.log` greps clean for `Upload failed`/`✗` — the cadence check's own error probe was blind to it. Proposed: drop the `✓` for skipped runs and emit a distinct token, count consecutive client-skips, and alert via `lib/ops-alert.js` past a threshold; mirror the threshold check into the supply-cadence task so the monitor cannot be fooled by a green log again. | P1 | BUILD-1 + BUILD-4 | **(a) REGRESSED 2026-08-28 — REOPENED. The 08-27 resolution held for about nine hours and did not survive the machine migration.** Restored that morning (real extraction `client-ead5d628…`, held=2, queue drained). Last REAL extraction `2026-08-27T19:08Z`. The new MacBook's setup completed `2026-08-27T20:18` and **Claude Code CLI credentials live in the Keychain, which the transition bundle explicitly did NOT carry** (RESTORE.md §1: "Keychain is NOT in this bundle... a clean install does not"). Since the migration: **2 client-skips, 0 real extractions.** Direct probe 2026-08-28: `claude -p` → `Failed to authenticate: OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed`, **exit 0** — the silent-success shape all over again. Surfaced not by the extraction monitor but by `com.slam.ops-consumer` logging `Invalid API key · Please run /login` on an unrelated job. **This class has now run silent TWICE in three weeks (2026-08-03→08-06, and 2026-08-27→now), and both times the `✓` in extract.log actively concealed it.** Tyler action: run `claude` interactively and `/login`, then confirm the next sweep logs a non-`client-skip` extraction id. **(a) RESOLVED (again) 2026-08-29 04:43 — verified end to end, and the auth failure was masking a SECOND, worse fault.** Operator ran `claude auth login`; `claude auth status` now `loggedIn:true` (claude.ai, tyler@slamagency.com). **But the first clean sweep then failed 14/14 uploads with `410 Server-side extraction is disabled`** — the vendored `~/.auxilo/bin/scripts/runner.js` had been silently DOWNGRADED to a pre-client-side-migration build (0 client-side markers, POSTing to the deprecated `/extract`), by a re-vendor at 2026-08-28 17:59 that also dropped the hook's `AUXILO_SCORE_EXTRACTION` line and reintroduced the pre-0.9.7 `--status` hook false-negative. **Root cause of the 17:59 re-vendor is UNKNOWN and worth watching — if it recurs it re-breaks supply.** Fixed by re-vendoring `scripts/`, `lib/`, `config/` from the published **auxilo-mcp@0.9.9**, local tarball shasum `dd1e22e87918eea7e84857fdf15a718b14107c7e` verified byte-identical to the Gate-A-reviewed tarball on record; shell wrappers deliberately left in place so the restored env line survived; prior state backed up to `~/.auxilo/bin.backup-20260828-downgraded`. Post-fix sweep: **10 discovered / 3 processed / 0 failed**, real extraction id `client-6aa5e338…` with the modern `client-side extraction:` line, `--status` reports `Hook installed: yes` again. **Lesson: an auth outage can hide an unrelated pipeline fault, because both terminate in the same `client-skip` bucket — which is exactly what (b) exists to separate.** **NEW SKIP CLASS FOUND during the same verification, spec updated (§2.3b): the skip path swallows MODEL ERRORS too.** On a healthy authenticated CLI, a malformed transcript returned `API Error: 400 … tool_use ids must be unique` and the runner stamped the identical `✓ … client-skip`. `client-skip` is therefore an undifferentiated bucket over at least three causes (unauthenticated / per-session model error / other non-zero exit); only the first starves the pipeline, and only it should drive the alert. Spec now requires distinct reason codes. Spec also gained §2.3a: use `claude auth status` (`loggedIn` bool) as the authoritative signal rather than parsing error strings — verified during the outage, it read `loggedIn:false` while `claude -p` was still exiting 0. **SEQUENCING RECOMMENDATION (PM, 2026-08-28, now stronger): promote (b) to NEXT, ahead of GOV2-DEL.** It is a small logging/counter/alert build, it has now failed twice with multi-day silent windows, and it is the only thing that makes a third occurrence visible without an unrelated job happening to leak the symptom. R13-CLOSE is mid-build and is NOT to be interrupted (one build in flight); this takes the slot after it.** (b) OPEN — BUILD-SPEC authored 2026-08-27 (`~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-EXT-0806b-SILENT-SKIP-2026-08-27.md`, base pinned at handoff): distinct `⊘ extraction SKIPPED` token replacing the lying `✓`, persistent consecutive-skip counter, ops-alert at threshold 2 with daily dedup, `--status` extraction line, supply-cadence probe mirror (PM-owned post-merge). | INSTALL-0828 | **The documented install path can silently deliver a JUNE build to a returning user, and it fails as a total no-op.** Root-caused 2026-08-28 after the operator ran the site's documented `npx auxilo setup` on a new MacBook and got a runner that 410'd every upload. Fingerprinted by sha against every published tarball: the installed runner is **auxilo-mcp@0.9.0** (2026-06-12), i.e. PRE-client-side-migration — it POSTs to the deprecated `/extract`, which the server 410s by design, so **every learning fails to upload while the log stamps `✓`**. **NOT a defect in the published packages** — verified: a clean-cache `npm install auxilo@0.9.0` resolves `auxilo-mcp` to **0.9.9** correctly, and the wrapper is a clean thin delegator (`auxilo-mcp: ^0.9.0`, no bundling). **The defect is the interaction of npx tree-caching with a wrapper that has not been republished since June.** `~/.npm/_npx` caches the whole RESOLVED tree keyed on the spec `auxilo`; the operator's June-era entries (two at `auxilo-mcp@0.9.0`, plus 0.8.1/0.8.2) migrated with the home directory, so `npx auxilo setup` reused a June tree instead of resolving fresh. **`npx auxilo@latest setup` would NOT have helped: the wrapper's own latest IS still 0.9.0** (published 2026-06-12, never rerolled), so the cache key is unchanged. **Blast radius: any user who tried Auxilo before ~2026-06-13 and later re-runs the documented setup command.** New users on a clean machine are fine. The failure is silent per the EXT-0806 class. **Interim (done 2026-08-28):** the four stale auxilo trees were deleted from `~/.npm/_npx` on the operator machine; four 0.9.9 entries remain and the vendored bin was re-installed from the verified 0.9.9 tarball. **Durable fix requires a wrapper republish** (raise the floor to `auxilo-mcp: ^0.9.9` and bump `auxilo` to 0.9.1 so the npx cache key changes) — **blocked on the dead npm token**, same blocker as EXT-0806b. Recommended companion, and cheap: have `auxilo setup` print the resolved `auxilo-mcp` version and refuse or loudly warn below a known-good floor, so a stale tree announces itself instead of silently no-op'ing. | P1 | BUILD-1 + SPEC-3 | **CLOSED 2026-08-29 — PUBLISHED + LIVE-VERIFIED.** `auxilo@0.9.1` published by Tyler; registry `dist.shasum` `38b2fd51ee3cca9b237235b0fbadd2cafb34bbbe` is byte-identical to the PM-packed tarball, published floor confirmed `auxilo-mcp: ^0.9.9`, `latest` now 0.9.1. **End-to-end proof on the real documented path:** a clean-cache `npm install auxilo` (what `npx auxilo setup` resolves) now pulls wrapper 0.9.1 → auxilo-mcp **0.9.9** with the client-side extraction path present. The June-build trap is closed for new AND returning users. Prepared and verified as follows: npm auth restored (`npm whoami` = s--architects--). `auxilo@0.9.1` staged at `~/dev/auxilo-wrapper/` — packed `38b2fd51ee3cca9b237235b0fbadd2cafb34bbbe`, 918 bytes, exactly the 3 published files. Reconstructed from the published 0.9.0 tarball verified byte-identical to the registry first (`dist.shasum` `66557d2763c77af0…`); `cli.js` and `README.md` UNCHANGED; `package.json` differs by exactly two lines (`0.9.0`→`0.9.1`, `auxilo-mcp ^0.9.0`→`^0.9.9`). **Proof it works: a clean-cache install of the packed tarball resolves auxilo-mcp to 0.9.9 with the client-side path present (7 markers).** The version bump is what changes the npx cache key so stale June trees stop being reused; the floor prevents recurrence. Site copy needs NO change — `npx auxilo setup` stays correct once the wrapper is rerolled. **NEW FINDING while preparing this: the `auxilo` wrapper had NO SOURCE CHECKOUT ANYWHERE** — published once 2026-06-12 and existing only as a registry tarball, which is the root enabler of this whole class (nothing to review, nothing to rebuild, no reason anyone would notice the stale floor). Now given a durable home at `~/dev/auxilo-wrapper/` with `PROVENANCE.md` recording the reconstruction and the exact diff. **Open Tyler call: whether the wrapper becomes a tracked subdirectory of the PUBLIC repo (reviewable, Gate-A'd, but a second package in the repo) or stays a separate private source of record.** Until decided, publish from that directory, never from a scratch dir. Companion still open (needs an auxilo-mcp release, so it rides EXT-0806b or later): have `auxilo setup` print the resolved auxilo-mcp version and warn/refuse below a known-good floor, so a stale tree announces itself instead of silently no-op'ing. |

| GRANT-TOOL | **Operator publication-trust grant path — DEPLOY BLOCKER for R13-CLOSE.** R13-CLOSE §2.2 requires an explicit operator grant to the operator's own account at deploy, so the dogfood flow survives the publication-authority gate. PM verified at `db87d56`: `grantPublicationTrust` (lib/publication-authority.js:17) has exactly ONE caller, server.js:10082 inside admin learning-approval. No route, no script, no npm entry. The only in-band alternative is hand-editing `data/accounts.json` on the live Fly volume, which **races every whole-file `saveAccounts()` rewrite** — unacceptable for a security-primitive mutation on prod. Build also closes two gaps GOV-3 named: **no revoke path** (contrast clean-lane, which has grant+revoke+auto-freeze) and **no audit trail** (contrast `appendCleanLaneRow`; note admin-conferred trust is currently the ONLY production grant path and it leaves no trace at all). Scope: `scripts/publication-trust.js` with grant/revoke/status/list, all mutations INSIDE `acquireAccountLock` with a re-`loadAccounts()`; a `grantor` field added additively (`isPublicationTrusted` must keep accepting legacy `admin_approval` records that lack it — test-pinned); refuse silent overwrite of an existing grant without `--force` (GOV-3 F-8: a later admin_approval currently clobbers the deploy-time operator_grant record wholesale); append-only `data/publication-trust-log.jsonl` written from the script AND the admin call site; and a per-row `approval_grants_publication_trust` flag on the admin moderation queue so an operator is warned that a routine approve click confers permanent trust (GOV-3 F-2). Spec: `~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-GRANT-TOOL-2026-08-29.md`, **base pinned `db87d56` (the R13-CLOSE tip, NOT origin/main — this build depends on lib/publication-authority.js which exists only there)**, relay-ready. Mandatory live probe: grant → clean self-approve succeeds → revoke → self-approve refused 409. | P1 | GOV-3 + BUILD-2 | **DESCOPED 2026-08-29 from deploy-blocking to FOLLOW-UP after a builder STOP that was SUSTAINED.** The builder refused rev 1 §2.1 as unbuildable and was right: `acquireAccountLock` is a module-scoped promise-chain mutex (`_accountMutexes`, server.js:4244), so a separate `node scripts/...` process gets its own empty map and cannot serialize against the live server's `saveAccounts()`. Extracting it to a module gives one mutex per process. The spec's own concurrency test would have proved only same-process serialization. PM verified. Ruled: when built, use an admin-authenticated server endpoint owning lock→reload→mutate→audit→save in the live process (precedent: /admin/settle, /admin/stage-key, /admin/moderation/:id/approve); CLI optional and only if it adds no second auth path. Grantor semantics ruled: `mechanism:admin_moderation` for admin-conferred trust, prefix REQUIRED so it cannot be misread as a person; `isPublicationTrusted` must never start requiring the field (legacy records lack it). Remaining scope, all still wanted: revoke path, append-only `data/publication-trust-log.jsonl` from BOTH the endpoint and the admin call site, admin-queue `approval_grants_publication_trust` warning. Spec rev 2 §-1 carries the full ruling. |

| ADMIN-TOKEN-0829 | **PROD HAS NO ADMIN TOKEN — every `/admin/*` route returns 401, and this makes R13-CLOSE's entire operator-review half inoperable on deploy.** Found 2026-08-29 while verifying the GRANT-TOOL STOP. `lib/admin-auth.js:39` requires `AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN`; PM verified it is set NOWHERE — absent from `flyctl secrets list -a auxilo` AND from `fly.toml`. Live probe: `GET /admin/reports` returns **401 with no token and 401 with any token**, because the comparison target is undefined. **This is a deploy-configuration prerequisite, NOT a code defect** — the code is correct, the secret was simply never provisioned, and it went unnoticed because no admin route appears to have been exercised on prod. Blast radius on R13-CLOSE specifically: (a) **R3-B holds a new contributor's first publication for operator review**, performed at `/admin/moderation/:id/approve` — unreachable; (b) **§3.2 report auto-hide alerts ops, who reviews at `/admin/reports`** — unreachable, so a reported item hides and no human can act on it; (c) **admin-conferred publication trust** (server.js:10082, the ONLY production grant path) — unreachable. R13-CLOSE would ship its human-in-the-loop half dead on arrival. **RULED: provisioning `AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN` is now THE R13-CLOSE deploy blocker, and it supersedes GRANT-TOOL's blocker status.** Once provisioned, §2.2's mandated operator grant is performable with NO new code via the existing admin-approval path (operator's first post-deploy extraction is held → operator approves it → `grantPublicationTrust` fires with `source:'admin_approval'`, `ref:'learning:<id>'`). PM ruling: `admin_approval` satisfies §2.2 in substance and is strictly MORE auditable than a bare `operator_grant`, since the ref points at a specific reviewed learning. **Tyler action: `flyctl secrets set AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN=<generated> -a auxilo`** (note a secret-set triggers a machine restart; fold it into the R13 deploy rather than doing it separately). Consider also `AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` — supported by `lib/admin-auth.js:27` and currently unset, so tokens never expire. PM owns the RUNBOOK entry (docs/RUNBOOK.md is absent/untracked). | P0 | GOV-3 + BUILD-2 | OPEN — blocks the R13-CLOSE DEPLOY (not the merge). Tyler action, config not build. **CLOSED 2026-08-29 — token provisioned + live-verified.** `AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN` set as a Fly secret (256-bit hex), machine restarted to v66 clean, `/admin/reports` returns 200 with the real token and 401 with a junk control token. All three previously-unreachable surfaces are now operable: R3-B first-publication operator review (`/admin/moderation/:id/approve`), §3.2 report triage (`/admin/reports`), and the admin-approval publication-trust grant path. **R13-CLOSE deploy is UNBLOCKED.** Residual, non-blocking: `AUXILO_ADMIN_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` is still unset, so the token never expires — code supports it (`lib/admin-auth.js:27`); worth a rotation forcing-function later. Documentation of the token's existence, purpose, and rotation procedure is owed to RUNBOOK (absent/untracked, PM-owned). *(Provenance: token set + positive 200 probe = Tyler directly; secret digest `d1ee7e40e3979716`, both 401 controls, and the v66 restart re-verified independently by TECH-PM.)* |

## 30. Catalog Identity — TECHNICAL-ONLY SCOPE (Tyler, 2026-07-19; SUPERSEDED same day: curation → hard scope)

**Decision (final form, Tyler 2026-07-19 evening):** non-technical learnings are OUT OF SCOPE for Auxilo entirely —
**not collected, not considered, not published, by any user.** Rationale: buyers are agents searching for technical
solutions to token-heavy challenges mid-task; non-tech search demand is unlikely. The earlier same-day tech-first
CURATION rule (approval priority + 20% share cap) is superseded by this hard scope rule.

**AMENDED 2026-07-26 (Tyler, SPEC3-G1 scope ruling): PARTIAL SUPERSESSION.** The hard scope stands UNCHANGED for the
public catalog and every buyer-facing surface (the rationale above is buyer-demand-based and untouched). The SPEC3-G1
**private** tier — owner-only, never visible to any other account, zero buyer surface — ACCEPTS non-technical learnings:
CI-5 is re-scoped as a marketplace-quality rule enforced at the promote-to-public boundary (sanitize → full screening
chain, where non-technical still 400s). "Not collected" is relaxed ONLY for private-destined, owner-opt-in capture
(backlog drain + private-lane runner mode); nothing non-technical can reach any public or buyer surface. Record:
`~/.auxilo/handoffs/BUILD-SPEC-SPEC3-G1-2026-07-26.md`.

**Executed 2026-07-19:** 138 pending non-tech items rejected (communication 32, content-generation 106, incl. the
sensitive-comms family); 9 live non-tech items retracted (6 via API within window, 3 via operator box-edit past
window + restart); live non-tech = 1 (lrn_resend01, a TECHNICAL email-API learning mislabeled `communication` —
kept, recategorization owed to the CI-5 enforcement build). Catalog: 68 live, effectively 100% technical.

| # | Rule | Owner | Status |
|---|------|-------|--------|

## 35. Public-Site Art-Director Visual Review (2026-08-28)

Source: `~/.auxilo/handoffs/AD-VISUAL-SITE-REVIEW.md` (FINAL, 44KB). Sequenced AFTER §34 and gated on it — the copy panel's settled messaging direction is the standard the visuals were judged against, and every row below names the copy finding it blocks or completes. Lead seat brand-art-director; supporting seats responsive-css-engineer and visual-brand-qa (source-level), whose load-bearing claims the lead then re-verified against the live DOM. Scope: the 8 live pages at 375 / 768 / 1440px. 34 findings (V1-V34). 38 real screenshots captured with headless Chrome at `~/.auxilo/handoffs/ad-visual-shots/`.

**Method note.** Numbers are computed styles, bounding rects and scroll extents read from the running site, not estimated from images; contrast ratios were computed from the token values. Provenance is marked per row: *lead-measured* (live), *both* (source-derived by a supporting seat, then live-verified by the lead), or *source-only* (not reproduced live, labelled latent).

**PM note on two self-corrections.** The lead's first pass made two wrong calls that the supporting seats caught and that were re-verified before filing, because both change the fix: (1) the breakpoint count is **7 across 35 media blocks**, not the 5 visible in `styles.css`; (2) the heading-size chaos is **not** "the site mixes fluid and fixed type" — the fluid ramp is consistent and well built, and the real cause is ancestor selectors carrying different clamp formulas over identical markup. The withdrawn hypothesis is recorded here so nobody re-derives it.

**What the review found working, so nothing here gets "fixed":** contrast passes AA/AAA at every pairing actually used (ivory/obsidian 18.94:1, slate/obsidian 6.29:1, obsidian-on-gold buttons 8.66:1; the one failing palette pair, ivory-on-gold at 2.19:1, is used nowhere); **zero horizontal-scroll violations** on any page at any width (`overflow-x: clip` at `styles.css:29` verified `scrollWidth === clientWidth` on all 8); the fluid type ramp; the `--section-pad` 120/80/64 step; container discipline on 7 of 8 pages; `aria-expanded` correct on every hamburger with a 46x44 target and 56px menu links; and every internal link and `#anchor` resolving (zero 404s, zero dead anchors).

**No file in `public/` was edited and nothing was pushed** — `public/` is surfaced-first per standing rule, so every row below is a proposal awaiting Tyler.

| ID | Item | Priority | Owner | Status |
|----|------|----------|-------|--------|
| AD-1 | **BLOCKER V19 — trust-critical copy is horizontally clipped on phones, and this gates COPY-18.** At 375px code blocks render a **293px box against up to 744px of content** (for-builders) and 514px (api); `overflow-x:auto` on `<pre>` means the page never scrolls sideways, so the defect is invisible to any whole-page check. Clipped strings include `# Approve what goes live: nothing publishes until you do`, `# installs the extraction runner, then asks consent (default: No)`, and the withdrawals-accrual disclosure. On api, **6 of 8** code blocks clip. **Cross-reference corrected 2026-08-29 after the COPY-9 direction reversal (`18a7aff`).** This row as first filed called the clipped line "the sentence F1 corrects"; that was wrong in the same way the original COPY-9 was. Per the behavioral check against prod, the clean lane is flag-dark, so **that line is ACCURATE today** — which makes the defect a different and still-live one: **a currently-true, load-bearing consent promise is only ~40% readable on a phone without scrolling inside the block.** **The measurement is unaffected by the reversal** — clipping is a rendering fact, verified from computed geometry, and holds whichever side of the contradiction is false. **The dependency moves to COPY-18:** when `EXTRACTION_AUTOPUBLISH_CONSENT_ENABLED` flips (D1/C1, counsel-gated), this code comment becomes FALSE and must be rewritten in that same wave — and it would be rewritten into a container that clips on mobile. So the layout fix must land with the flip, not after it. Consent and payment-state disclosures belong in prose beneath the block, not inside code comments; that is a layout decision, not a copy edit. | P1 | BUILD-3 + BUILD-1 | **OPEN 2026-08-28 — lead-measured live** (box vs content widths and clipped strings read from the running DOM at 375px). Hard precondition on COPY-18 (the consent-flip wave) landing correctly; independently a live mobile-readability defect on an accurate disclosure today. |
| AD-2 | **BLOCKER V10 — the button hierarchy is inverted and the primary action is styled as the weakest control on the page.** Computed: `btn-primary` gold fill / obsidian text / 15px / **52px** tall; `btn-secondary` transparent / ash border 0.2 alpha / 15px / **54px** tall; `copy-btn` transparent / slate border / **11px** type / `2px 8px` padding / **36px** tall. The secondary is **2px taller than the primary**, and the control that copies `npx auxilo setup` — the whole conversion thesis per COPY-12/F5 — is the smallest, lowest-contrast, lowest-type-size interactive element in the system. This is the visual half of F5 and **no copy rewrite fixes it**. Proposal: real primary treatment on the install block (gold fill, 52px, full-width at 375px), demote today's `btn-primary` destinations to secondary, pin `btn-secondary` to 52px so it can never out-measure primary. | P1 | BUILD-3 | **OPEN 2026-08-28 — lead-measured live.** Pairs with COPY-12. |

| WAL-ORPHAN | **An interrupted withdrawal's WAL entry is DELETED at the next boot without ever being replayed — the WAL failing at its one job, on the money path.** `recoverWalEntries` (server.js:1404) reads ALL pending entries, replays only `unlock` (:1411) and `pipeline_approve` (:1412), then calls `commitWal(entry.id)` at :1414 UNCONDITIONALLY — deleting `withdraw` and `withdraw_stripe` entries it never replayed. Their handlers live only in the dedicated scan at :1909-2067, which re-reads the directory AFTER the delete and finds nothing. CONSEQUENCE: the entire withdrawal-recovery block is unreachable on a normal boot. It fires only on entries created BETWEEN the two scans — which is exactly the race that produced the 1-in-4 baseline crash, and why WAL-TDZ looked intermittent rather than structural. EMPIRICALLY PROVEN on an isolated staged copy (lib/ copied, not symlinked): seeded one `withdraw` entry debiting \$5 against a \$10 pending balance; boot logged `[wal-recovery] Found 1 pending WAL entries`, the WAL directory was EMPTY afterwards, and earnings were untouched (total_withdrawn 0, withdrawal_count 0, processed_settlements empty). POSITIVE CONTROL: with the generic scan disabled the same entry DID reach the loop and executed, proving the loop works and the generic scan is what starves it. SEQUENCING (load-bearing): fixing this ALONE, without the WAL-TDZ hoist, converts silent money-path data loss into a boot crash on every restart with a pending withdrawal — the two must land TOGETHER, hoist first. Conversely the WAL-TDZ dynamic RED test only becomes deterministic AFTER this is fixed. | WAL-ISOLATION | **Staged test servers are not sandboxed for the WAL — they share the real checkout's `data/wal/`** (same battery, same root cause chain): `lib/wal.js:23` sets `WAL_DIR = path.join(__dirname,'..','data','wal')`, and the shared stage/boot helper symlinks `lib/` to the real checkout, so a staged server's `__dirname` resolves WAL paths OUTSIDE its tmp sandbox. Consequences: tests write into the real checkout's data dir (test/wave5a-money-closures.test.js:499 creates a pending `wd_test` entry there), and parallel test files cross-contaminate — spec3-b1's boot replays wave5a's in-flight entry (→ WAL-TDZ). Fix: derive WAL_DIR from the server's DATA_DIR / an env override so staged servers stay inside their sandbox; add a pin that a staged server never touches the checkout's data/. FLAKE-RESID class. **SPEC STAMPED 2026-09-02 inside BUILD-SPEC-WAL-2026-09-02.md (Problem B):** `lib/wal.js` WAL_DIR honors `AUXILO_WAL_DIR`, then `AUXILO_DATA_DIR/wal` (the existing convention in five libs), then the __dirname fallback, + a read-only accessor; the staging helper defaults `AUXILO_WAL_DIR=<tmp>/data/wal`; the two in-process WAL writers (wave1 + wave5a money-closures) set the env to a per-file mkdtemp BEFORE requiring lib/wal.js; pin (i) unit resolution + (ii) staged boot leaves the checkout's data/wal listing unchanged. Defaulting AUXILO_DATA_DIR in the helper is explicitly OUT of scope (re-homes five other libs). Relay-ready with WAL-TDZ — **in the same Codex build (slot ruled 2026-09-02).** **DELIVERED with WAL-TDZ (`e6f56e2` → `c27ee52` → v81):** `lib/wal.js` WAL_DIR = `AUXILO_WAL_DIR` → `AUXILO_DATA_DIR/wal` → default (prod sets neither, verified in fly.toml/Dockerfile); `walDir()` accessor; staged-server creates `<tmp>/data/wal` and passes AUXILO_WAL_DIR to the child; wave1/wave5a direct-import tests point WAL at a temp dir and clean up; 2 isolation tests. PROOF: four consecutive full runs with zero boot failures; `data/wal/` in the build worktree empty after all of them. | P2 | BUILD-4 + BUILD-1 | CLOSED 2026-09-05 — deployed v81. |
| CP2-PROBABILISTIC | **`test/wave2b-ops-hardening.test.js:371-377` asserts that random ciphertext never spells a plaintext substring — an intrinsically probabilistic check** (first observed failure: main CI on 8827b4a, 2026-09-02, "ciphertext must not contain 'Doe'"; 13 prior visible runs green; local ×5 green). The test JSON-stringifies the AES-GCM record (base64 ciphertext + IV + tag) and asserts none of `Jane`/`Doe`/`United States`/`W-9` appear; a 3-char sequence in a few hundred base64 chars collides ~1 in 10³ runs. Pre-existing test-design flaw — unrelated to FLAKE-0728, WAL-TDZ, or SEED-ATTR. Fix: assert the leak-property directly (decrypt with the key round-trips; decrypt without the key fails; ciphertext ≠ plaintext bytes) instead of substring-hunting a random string, or use a sentinel long enough (≥12 chars) that collision is negligible. Until fixed, a CI red showing exactly this subtest is a rerun, not a stop. | P3 | BUILD-4 | OPEN 2026-09-02. |
| FLAKE-EADDRINUSE | **Staged-server harness retries the SAME port after `EADDRINUSE`, so a lost port-reservation race fails three times and the test fails.** Observed 2026-09-05 in Gate-A run 3 on the GOV2-DEL merge: `test/pricing-visibility.test.js:205` → `staged server boot failed on attempt 3 (EADDRINUSE :::54080)`; runs 1, 2, 4 and check-test-count green; BOOT_FAILED 0 (not the WAL race, which is fixed). `reservePort()` closes the probe socket before the child binds, and node:test runs files in parallel, so a sibling can take the port in between; `bootServer`'s retry re-uses the port baked into the staged server.js. Fix shape: on `EADDRINUSE`, re-reserve a fresh port and re-stage (or stage with `PORT=0` and read the bound port from the child's ready line). Distinct from FLAKE-0728 (boot timeouts) and CP2-PROBABILISTIC. | P3 | BUILD-4 | OPEN 2026-09-05; one occurrence; re-run policy applies until fixed. |

| PARTITION-GUARD | **The trust page's §4 candor block states a frozen catalog partition (`Forty-five…` platform-attributed / `The rest…` operator-account / `No outside builder has published here yet`) while §7 renders a live count on the same page — a ship-time re-derivation does not survive ship: a 46th null-account row or the FIRST external contributor silently falsifies §4 at the moment that milestone lands, on the page a new partner is most likely reading.** (SITE-PM finding 5, both its gates.) **TECH-PM RULING ON SHAPE (2026-09-02, ownership mine): the DURABLE shape is feasible with the shipped pattern and is the requirement, not the option.** (1) `/how-submissions-work` gets the `/earnings` route shape (server.js ~:11362): ONE partition function over `visibleCatalog()` selects the candor block's branch server-side by id-anchored substitution — post-rev-3 the block carries NO numbers (GTM rev 9 dropped "Forty-five" from prose; SITE-PM declined an inline live number, prose-only by its call), so the only live thing in §4 is the branch (State A / State B) plus a `data-partition-state="A|B|none"` marker the probe reads; live counts stay inside the §7 card; (2) the same function feeds a new `composition` object on `/knowledge/stats` (`platform_attributed`, `internal_account`, `external_contributors`, `null_account`) so §4 and §7 draw from one computation and the claim is machine-verifiable — GTM note: AGGREGATE attribution becomes API-visible while per-row attribution stays store-level, so the candor line "attribution is store-level, not API-visible" re-scopes to per-row; (3) PREDICATE (hardened twice on 2026-09-02, both from GTM catches, both verified in code): the code has NO operator concept (only `PLATFORM_ACCOUNT_IDS` lib/accounts.js:139 and `PLATFORM_WALLETS` = `WALLET` + `LEGACY_PLATFORM_WALLETS` server.js:314-319). INTERNAL row = `isPlatformContributor(row, PLATFORM_WALLETS)` (the existing money-side predicate — already lowercases wallets, already treats a null-account/null-wallet row as platform; NO second platform definition, no-fork rule) OR operator row = `contributor_account_id` ∈ env operator account ids OR lowercased `contributor_wallet` ∈ env operator wallets (REGISTERED HOME, ruled 2026-09-02 after GTM's ninth-gate finding: a TRACKED config file `config/internal-identities.json` — public-safe, it holds only public wallet addresses and account ids, no secrets; the operator wallet is already public in CLAUDE.md and on-chain, the account id already in this file — loaded beside `PLATFORM_WALLETS`, with env `AUXILO_INTERNAL_IDENTITIES` as a per-environment ADDITIVE override only, never the sole home). EXTERNAL = every other visible row — which correctly includes a wallet-only outside submission (post-apply every legacy-wallet row carries `acc_platform`; a new bare-wallet row is a server.js "wallet-only orphan", adoptable on wallet link per AUD19-3) and correctly EXCLUDES an account-less submission from Auxilo's own wallets (GTM's false-trigger catch: the 45 seed rows are exactly that shape and 16 operator-account rows carry Auxilo wallets today — 14 on 0xA19C, 2 legacy — so an accounts-only internal set would flip State B with no outside builder in existence). Wallet comparison is lowercased (the :8110 CONTRIB-STAT casing class). Standing principle (GTM, adopted for Gate-A on this build): where copy renders unattended on a data predicate, the predicate is gated as tightly as the sentence — the predicate IS the claim's only guard at flip time. FAIL-CLOSED: env unset → state "none", the zero-external sentence is OMITTED, never asserted (and the probe fires, see 6b); (4) the static HTML carries NO partition literals — placeholders only — so a render failure can never serve a stale `Forty-five` or a stale zero-external (the fa :599 literal-fallback lesson); (5) copy = a two-branch template (zero-external / has-external), each branch Tyler-gated ONCE; GTM composes the has-external branch before the slot; (6) standing signal with zero new infrastructure: the `uptime` workflow (GitHub infra, every 30 min, emails repo watchers on failure) gains one probe on `composition` — FAIL on `external_contributors` departing its pinned value (the pin is edited when the milestone lands, which IS the review); `null_account` is REPORTED and counted inside external (wallet-only), NOT treated as a regression — the earlier "null_account > 0 = regression" rule was wrong and is withdrawn (see (3)); (6b, SITE-PM addition adopted) the probe ALSO fetches the served page and FAILS when §4 renders with NEITHER branch (`data-partition-state="none"`: env unset or partition unreadable) — fail-closed on the claim, LOUD on the seat, because a silent omission of State A would drop the strongest sentence of a block Tyler ruled full-candor on with no reader-visible symptom; GTM's campaign tripwire fires on the same first-external event (belt and braces); (7) a pin test seeds platform/operator/external rows and asserts the partition and the rendered branch — INCLUDING the false-positive case as a BUILD-TIME VERIFICATION before ship (SITE-PM precondition, 2026-09-02): a null-account row bearing an internal wallet resolves INTERNAL, because a false external flip would make the page ASSERT an untruth ("An outside builder has published here" with none — GTM's earlier plural string is STRUCK by SITE-PM at rev 3f on GTM's tenth gate: a plural subject asserts at least two at an external count of one) on the one page whose job is being checkable — worse than the fail-closed omission of a true sentence. LOAD-BEARING NOTE (GTM catch 2026-09-02, sharpened by TECH-PM at code): `isPlatformContributor` ALONE classifies ALL 171 operator-account rows EXTERNAL — clause (b) returns false for any non-platform account id, and clause (a) only early-returns on a NON-platform wallet, so even the 16 operator rows carrying Auxilo wallets fall through to (b) (GTM counted 155; it is 171). The operator arm of the OR is therefore the SOLE thing keeping the operator catalog internal: the build ships a code comment saying exactly that beside the predicate, and the fixture test enumerates every shape (operator-account no-wallet / operator-account + 0xA19C / operator-account + legacy wallet / `acc_platform` / wallet-only outside / outside account) so a future "simplification" to `isPlatformContributor` alone turns CI red instead of flipping State B falsely on day one. The internal==216 / external==0 assertion is the PRE-SHIP partition re-derivation (precondition 5), not a CI pin — it stales at the first outside publication by design. ALLOWLIST RULE (GTM ninth gate, evidence verified by TECH-PM on the 09-02 backup): the internal-identity register is demonstrably open-ended — the 2026-08-30 R13 probe row `lrn_eb4c3040…` carries a THIRD Auxilo-side wallet `0xf963c3…02fb` (status rejected, null account, so zero live impact; the whole store holds 5 distinct wallets — legacy, 0xA19C, the probe, two retracted junk wallets — and the VISIBLE set exactly 2) and the predicate classifies any unregistered wallet EXTERNAL. Rule, recorded in RUNBOOK at build: every Auxilo-side wallet or account is added to the register BEFORE first use, probe identities included, and probe artifacts are NEVER approved (a probe that simulates an outsider is correctly external; approving one would flip State B falsely). Made explicit in the same place: null-account rows are EXTERNAL (server.js:6212 — `contributor_account_id` is "null only for bare wallet submissions"), or an approved outside bare-wallet publication would leave State A rendering false. And `external_contributors` in the composition object derives from the partition as DISTINCT IDENTITIES (account id when present, else lowercased wallet) — never from a wallet Set (see CONTRIB-STAT's second defect). GTM TENTH GATE (2026-09-02, adopted): (8) TWO fields, not one — `external_contributors` (IDENTITY count from the shared distinct-identity helper; GTM's first-external tripwire) AND `external_learnings` (ROW count; the trust page's magnitude sentence if Tyler picks that option) — nothing named "contributors" ever reaches the page (ruling 11); (9) PRECEDENCE: the Auxilo-wallet allowlist check DOMINATES the null-account rule — a no-account row with an Auxilo-side wallet is INTERNAL; compounding hazard: `visibleCatalog()`'s moderation-OFF branch (server.js:3251-3260) excludes only pending rows with platform holds, so if `CONTENT_MODERATION_ENABLED` is ever false a rejected public row enters the partition — the probe row `lrn_eb4c3040…` (rejected, null account, wallet 0xf963c…) is exactly that shape today, so that wallet is named explicitly in `config/internal-identities.json` at creation; (10) VALUE CLAUSES (binding only if Tyler picks the magnitude option): branch and value derive from the SAME evaluation in the SAME request (a split read renders "all but 0" inside the has-external branch), and a failed substitution suppresses the WHOLE sentence, never an empty span. The has-external branch is ADDITIVE (gate-clean first sentence + optional magnitude sentence), so the build proceeds before Tyler's magnitude ruling. | SUBSTITUTION-RULE | **STANDING VERIFICATION RULE, adopted 2026-09-02 across all three seats after the PRICE-ENGINE finding: a substitution is a NEW claim and inherits NONE of the old claim's verification.** Origin: the PRICE-008 correction retired a false constant ("$0.08 default starting price") and replaced it with "priced by the engine", which is itself false on a shipped path.
