SITE-PM caught it. The fix was not more runs: the failure is LOAD-SENSITIVE, so measuring it under added measurement load yields a number that must be caveated into uselessness — matched N would have bought a worse-looking version of the same uncertainty. **The empirical claim was DROPPED and the causal claim rests on mechanism alone (the wave touches zero non-public files; a copy-only change cannot alter a server's module-load order), which is categorical and needs no runs.** Reporting form that follows: state what WAS measured, state explicitly what was NOT, and name the unsupported comparison as not-evidence rather than omitting it — silence lets a reader supply the inference themselves. **The generalization: this is the same defect the day was spent killing in the copy — technically defensible, practically misleading — except the failure moves from the page into the INSTRUMENT.** **(2) A RULE THAT HAS TO BE ARGUED INTO ITS FIRST CASE IS USUALLY THE WRONG RULE.** Origin: the cross-seat scope boundary was tightened, and rather than assume the narrower form, SITE-PM tested it against the hardest live case — which qualified independently, on contradiction, costing nothing. Had the tighter rule needed special pleading to reach a case all three seats already agreed on, the RULE would have been wrong rather than the case exceptional. | P2 | GOV-1 | STANDING 2026-09-02. |
| PRICE-BAND | **The "every reprice stays between 50% and 300% of base" claim is LIVE on prod and FALSE — a THIRD live pricing class, found by the narrow rev-3 gate and confirmed two independent ways (the gate by EXECUTING the pricing code, TECH-PM by reading it).** Two independent mechanisms break it: **(a) the cron's damping is applied AFTER the band clamp and never re-clamps.** `lib/pricing.js:352-354` clamps `price` to `[base×0.5, base×3.0]`, but `runDailyPricingCron` (server.js:9439-9445) then rate-limits the move to ±15%/day from `oldPrice` and writes that damped value — so when `oldPrice` sits outside the band the written price stays outside it. Gate's executed example: stored `base_price 0.08` with a builder override `current_price 50` reprices to **42.50**, against a band of [0.04, 0.24] — 531× outside, and it remains outside for roughly 33 cron days. **(b) the `base` the clamp uses is NOT the stored `base_price`.** In the cron path `getCurrentPrice` re-derives it via `calculateLearningPrice` (lib/pricing.js:339-341) and the cron NEVER writes `base_price` back — grep confirms it is written only at submission and re-listing (server.js:6179, :6187, :10835). So the stored base_price is a stale submission snapshot and the served price walks away from it by design (the density-gate comment at lib/pricing.js:330-337 says so): gate's executed example with NO override at all, `base_price 0.08` → `current_price 0.4800` after 20 cron days = **600% of the stored base**. **Referent defect on top: on an override path `base_price` is the ENGINE's figure, not the builder's (server.js:6177-6181), while this same page now tells builders they set the price — so "the learning's base price" reads as the number the builder typed, and it is neither that number nor a bound on the served price.** LIVE INSTANCES (HEAD bytes + fetched from prod, which returns 4 hits on /pricing): `public/pricing.html:115` + `:1042` (FAQ twins), `:688` (the bounds callout), `:1013` (the "Price Bounds" econ card). Plus `AGENT-TEAM.md:212`, internal. Fix is SITE-PM's composition; the true shape is that each reprice is computed within 50-300% of a FRESHLY DERIVED base and then damped to ±15% a day, which is not a bound the reader can check against any number shown to them — so the honest form may be to state the daily-move cap and drop the band entirely. **A THIRD FALSE PRICING NUMBER, found the same hour by applying the execution rule to MY OWN pending strings — "up to 15% a day" is FALSE.** `server.js:9479-9481`: the reprice runs 30 seconds after EVERY server start (`setTimeout(runDailyPricingCron, 30000)`) AND every 24 hours (`setInterval(..., 24*60*60*1000)`). The ±15% clamp is therefore PER RUN, not per day, and every deploy or machine restart triggers another one. Evidence from today, not hypothetical: prod's uptime read 0.91 hours because I restarted the machine for the SEED-ATTR migration, so prod repriced at least twice today; a three-deploy day moves a learning ~75%, not 15%. **PROVENANCE, both directions: the band form came from GTM's register TRUE-SHAPE entry and was relayed to two seats as the wording to compose against (GTM retracted at source and recorded the propagation rather than deleting it); the "daily cap" replacement was MINE, given to SITE-PM and GTM as composition guidance one message after I told them a mechanism claim about a computed value wants EXECUTION rather than reading — I read the cron loop and stopped two lines above its scheduler. Both were contained only because the wave is held; neither reached a deploy.** **THREE FALSE PRICING NUMBERS IN ONE DAY, each passing a static read by multiple seats: the $0.08 default, the 50-300% band, the 15%-a-day cap.** TRUE FORM for composition: a reprice moves a price by at most 15% from its previous value, and a reprice happens daily and again shortly after every server restart. **SELF-APPLICATION FAILURE ALSO RECORDED: I corrected the cadence in ONE place in my own doc-fix file and left FOUR standing — the class-sweep failure, committed inside the document written to close a class. All five corrected; sweeping my own artifact is now part of the fix.** **GROUND TRUTH ESTABLISHED BY EXECUTION 2026-09-02 CT — TECH-PM built the trace harness GTM asked for (submission → 40 reprice runs, real `lib/pricing.js`, the cron's damping arithmetic reproduced verbatim from server.js:9436-9448 since the loop is not exported). Four cases, and they settle the copy question rather than continuing to argue it:** **(A) ordinary submission, no override, fresh base:** engine 0.72, converges to 0.76 by run 1 and holds — **INSIDE the band the whole way, so the band claim is TRUE for this case.** **(B) override at the $50 ceiling:** 50 → 42.50 → 36.13 → 30.71 …, **19 consecutive runs OUTSIDE the stored-base band [0.36, 2.16]**, re-entering only at run 20. **(C) override at the $0.05 floor:** 14 runs outside, re-entering at run 15. **(D) STALE stored base (0.08) while the engine's current view is 0.72** — the case GTM's gate hit: the price climbs to 0.76 and **STAYS at 950% of the stored `base_price` PERMANENTLY**, because the cron never rewrites `base_price`. Not a transient; it never re-enters. **CONCLUSION: the band holds ONLY when there is no override AND the stored base is current. It is false transiently on any override and false FOREVER on a stale base. It is therefore not a claim any surface can make.** **THE TRUE, CHECKABLE, SIMPLE BEHAVIOUR the harness reveals — recommended as the copy foundation, replacing both the band and the retracted daily-cap: the served price moves toward what the engine currently computes, by at most 15% per reprice run, from wherever it starts.** That is true in all four cases, needs no number the reader cannot see, and survives the override, stale-base, floor and ceiling paths alike. **FOURTH MOVER CONFIRMED: in every case the engine's target moved with ZERO demand, ratings or freshness change — the base is re-derived against the live catalog, so catalog composition alone moves price. The canonical form names three movers; the code has four.** Gate-vs-TECH-PM reconciliation on record: the gate reported 600% on its fixture, I measured 950% on mine — same mechanism, different stale-base magnitude, and the divergence is itself the point, since the overshoot scales with how stale the snapshot is. Harness is scratchpad-only and ephemeral by design; EXEC-VERIFY-AUDIT carries the method for the remaining claims. **RESIDUAL 2026-09-05 (git-ls-files sweep):** `AGENT-TEAM.md:212` still states `reprices bounded 50% to 300%` — tracked, public repo. Same fix class; ships in LAUNCH-WAVE (clause dropped; the doc is the 2026-03-25 org file GTM already flagged as stale canon). **RESIDUAL CLOSED 2026-09-05** (AGENT-TEAM.md:212 clause dropped in LAUNCH-WAVE, verified at raw main). | P2 | SITE-PM + SPEC-1 + BUILD-3 | OPEN 2026-09-02; live, blocks Wave 3 rev 4. |

GTM raised the generalization, TECH-PM converts it from a caution into a work item, because a caution living in three transcripts is not a control. **Why static reading fails on this class specifically: all three defects are in the ORDER or CADENCE of operations that are each individually correct — the band clamp is correct and the ±15% damping is correct, but the damping runs after and never re-clamps; the reprice interval is correct and the startup timer is correct, but together the cap is per-run not per-day. Deliberately NOT scoped to Wave 3; this is its own slot and it wants Tyler's call on when. **SECOND NAMED CLASS IN THIS AUDIT — ENUMERATION-BY-OMISSION (GTM 2026-09-02, adopted): a list of causes is false only by what it LEAVES OUT, and omission is precisely what gates do not catch — every check asks whether the named items are true, none asks whether the list is COMPLETE.** Origin: the movers enumeration (→ PRICE-MOVERS) passed BOTH Wave-3 gates and a narrow third gate, because all three named movers are individually true. Scope for the audit: every enumeration this property serves — the pricing movers, the four quality dimensions, the accepted-category list, the supported-client tiers, the split conditions, the review-gate steps. **Method that finds it: derive the complete set from code FIRST, then diff the copy's list against it — never read the copy's list and check each item, which is the procedure that produced the miss.** Companion class to the execution gap: one defeats reading, the other defeats checking. | P2 | SPEC-1 + BUILD-4 + GTM-PM | OPEN 2026-09-02. |

The earlier row-it ruling is SUPERSEDED SAME-SESSION and this row records the amendment as shipping, not as a trade that was made.** GTM's confirmation to both seats: the operative ruling is the GATE, and the gated string is `set initially by the builder or calculated by the engine from complexity, uniqueness, and quality, then moved by demand, freshness, ratings, and how the rest of the catalog changes`, tested against the formula item by item before gating. **THE DEFECT IT FIXES:** the base is re-derived against the live catalog on every reprice run (lib/pricing.js:339-341), so catalog composition alone moves a price with zero change in that learning's own demand, freshness or ratings — dominant on a stale-base learning (TECH-PM harness case D: settles at 950% of stored base, permanently). | SWEEP-INSTRUMENT | **MY NEGATIVES SWEEP WAS SILENTLY BROKEN AND REPORTED "ALL CLEAN" THREE TIMES ON TREES THAT WERE NOT — the most serious finding of this seat's session, and it is in my own instrument rather than in the product.** Mechanism: the sweep ran `n=$(grep -c -i -- "$pat" $P 2>/dev/null \| awk -F: '{s+=$2} END{print s+0}')` where `$P` held the nine filenames. **The shell is zsh, which does NOT word-split an unquoted parameter expansion** (unlike bash), so `$P` expanded to ONE filename containing spaces, grep exited 2 with "No such file or directory", stderr was swallowed by `2>/dev/null`, stdout was empty, awk summed nothing, and `print s+0` emitted **0** — which the sweep printed as CLEAN. **A failing check and a passing check produced byte-identical output.** Blast radius: every "full negatives sweep, all nine surfaces — no NONZERO = clean" I reported for Wave 3 rev 3, rev 4 and rev 5, to Tyler and to two peer seats. **What it actually hid, re-derived with a loud tool: the rev-5 tree carried `smart pricing` ×2, `in real time` ×2, `stale price` ×2 and `estimated discovery cost` ×2 — the defective for-builders tail, restored because the clean re-run lost two fixes I had applied BY HAND at rev 4 and never put into the staging script.** The narrow gate caught it; my sweep had cleared it forty minutes earlier. **REPLACEMENT INSTRUMENT (in use from now): a Python sweep that OPENS each file (so a missing or unreadable file raises rather than returning zero), counts literal case-insensitive occurrences, and prints per-file hits — no shell word-splitting, no swallowed stderr, no arithmetic that can turn an error into a zero.** **THE RULE, and it generalizes past this incident: a negative check must be able to FAIL LOUDLY. If an error and a clean result are indistinguishable in the output, the check cannot be evidence of absence — this is the same defect class as the `head`-truncated grep that appeared to confirm a phantom feature, and as `curl -sf` returning exit 0 on a 404 body, both already on this record. Three instances now, all in verification tooling rather than in code.** **SECOND INSTRUMENT FAILURE THE SAME DAY, SITE-PM's, and its shape is sharper than mine: its check for fully-bold standalone paragraphs returned ZERO property-wide and was the PRIMARY support for a ruling. The true count is one — `how-it-works:895` — because its pattern required a bare `<strong>` while the single existing instance in the whole corpus carries `<strong style="color:var(--ivory)">`. | DIY-COST-CLAIM | **`index.html:288` is LIVE on prod claiming a rediscovery cost the engine does not compute — found by the final Wave-3 verification sweep, immediately after GTM ruled the SAME TWO NUMBERS false on earnings and deleted them.** Live text: `This is real operational knowledge an agent found doing real work, the kind of thing that costs you 45 minutes and an estimated $3 in API calls to figure out yourself.` **Different claim, same non-existent basis.** The earnings sentence said the ENGINE prices from those numbers; this one says REDISCOVERY costs them. Verified at code by TECH-PM: `estimateDiyCost` (lib/pricing.js) returns `tokenCostSaved + timeValueSaved` — the same two category/complexity constants — as a DOLLAR figure, computing **$0.60** for a moderate web-interaction item, **$0.80** moderate code-execution, **$4.20** complex. **There is NO time estimate anywhere in the engine: `TIME_VALUE_SAVED` is denominated in dollars, not minutes.** So the word `estimated` points at a product estimate that computes no minutes at all, and the dollar figure does not match what it computes for a card of that kind. **Method note: this survived every sweep in the wave because the earlier greps targeted the pricing-MECHANISM vocabulary; it only surfaced when the post-deletion sweep searched for the deleted NUMBERS themselves — the claim-class rule again, where the class is the unsupported specific rather than the wording.** Disposition: SITE-PM's composition and Tyler's scope call. Arguments both ways stated rather than resolved — it sits on a page this wave already edits and it would be odd to delete a claim from earnings for having no basis while the identical figures keep serving on the front page, but folding it is a seventh scope expansion and SITE-PM has drawn that line twice today. **NOT touched by TECH-PM.** **STRENGTH OF THIS FINDING CORRECTED DOWN BY TECH-PM on SITE-PM's precision — my original framing overstated it, and letting it stand would have handed Tyler a stronger claim than the evidence supports, which is the exact failure this day was spent killing, committed in my own record.** The two sentences are NOT the same defect. **The earnings sentence sat inside a paragraph explicitly describing HOW THE ENGINE SETS PRICE, so it asserted the engine reasons from those numbers — demonstrably false, correctly deleted. The index sentence claims REDISCOVERY COSTS THE READER those numbers: a claim about the world, not about the product, and it does not become false merely because the engine computes something different.** **HONEST FINDING, weaker and still worth having: the `45 minutes` has NO product basis at all (no minutes computation exists anywhere in the engine; `TIME_VALUE_SAVED` is dollar-denominated at 0.04, 0.30, 1.20), and the `$3` is defensible only for a COMPLEX item, where `estimateDiyCost` returns $4.20, while sitting four to five times above what the estimator returns for a typical MODERATE one at $0.60 to $0.80. UNSUPPORTED AND INCONSISTENT, NOT FALSE.** The symmetry with the deleted earnings claim makes it FEEL stronger than it is; identical numbers suggest a shared origin but are not themselves evidence. **Consequence for the scope decision: deleting a false engine claim from earnings does not obligate anyone to touch a different, weaker claim on the front page inside the same wave. Whether live front-page copy moves at all is Tyler's decision, not a tidiness call.** | P2 | SITE-PM + GTM-PM | CLOSED 2026-09-05 — `index:288` gone since Wave 3 (live grep 0); formally closed in LAUNCH-WAVE. |

Removing them would leave an unusable rule and break a deliberate pairing between the instruction file and the server-side floor.** **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THE RULE OPERABLE, since "no numbers" alone would have stripped the working ones: a number describing the WORLD drifts and does not belong (prices, costs, catalog counts, durations); a number that IS the contract is definitional, enforced, and required for the agent to act. > **P2.1a — Bugs discovered during T-107/T-108 runs (2026-04-18), queued as P1 fast-follows**:
> - P1-1: `claude-code source.discoverSessions` looked in wrong path (`conversations/` subdir) — ✅ FIXED `2732ac6`.
> - P1-2: `claude-code source.readSession` extracted `msg.content` (wrong level) — ✅ FIXED `2732ac6`. Real shape is `msg.message.content` array of typed blocks.
> - P1-3: `runner.js --transcript <path>` handler missing — ✅ FIXED `ece8072`. Single-file mode now works via source adapter + scrub + POST pipeline; plain-text fallback when readSession fails.
> - P1-4: `ip_redacted` IPv4-only regex — ✅ FIXED `799c972`. New `redactIp()` helper handles IPv4 (mask last octet) + IPv6 (truncate at /64 prefix). Unit-tested in-place.
> - P1-5: `/extract` Idempotency-Key undocumented — ✅ FIXED `560adec`. AGENT-LEARNING-GUIDE §4 now has full request example + 8-row error-code cheat sheet.
> - P1-6: `/extract/consent` POST route missing — ✅ FIXED `799c972`. Thin wrapper over appendConsent with action→mode mapping (grant→automatic, revoke→off).
> - P1-7: `/account/settings` Bearer-JWT-only, rejects X-API-Key — ⏸ DEFERRED. Runner can't flip modes programmatically. Needs auth-surface review before changing; post-soak task.
> - P1-8: `runner.js` ignored `~/.auxilo/credentials.json` — ✅ FIXED `ece8072`. `loadCredentials()` now reads env → file → localhost default. Matches mcp-server.js.
> - P1-9: Extractor chunk failures silent — ✅ FIXED `dcd3d91`. Server now logs `X/Y chunks failed LLM extraction: <error>` to stderr when `providerResult.stats.chunks_failed > 0`.
> - P1-10: Dockerfile runs as root — ✅ FIXED `5a1cf91`. New `scripts/docker-entrypoint.sh` chowns /app/data then `exec su-exec node`. Applied on next Fly deploy.
> - P1-11: Anthropic dashboard truncated-key display confusion — pending doc note in AGENT-LEARNING-GUIDE (P2 polish).
> - P1-12: npm audit high advisories — ✅ FIXED `6cc3f73`. 4 advisories patched via in-major upgrades (hono, path-to-regexp, express-rate-limit, @hono/node-server). 0 vulnerabilities remaining.
> - P1-13: LaunchAgent `auxilo-sweeper` TCC-blocked on `~/Documents/` — ✅ DONE (2026-06-10). Root cause fixed in repo: new `node scripts/runner.js --install-sweeper` copies the full executable surface (wrapper + runner + sources + sensitivity-filter) to `~/.auxilo/bin/` and rewrites the plist to point there (`scripts/runner.js` installSweeper(), `scripts/auxilo-sweeper-wrapper.sh` now resolves runner relative to its own location). Also fixed a second latent bug found during the fix: wrapper + plist exported `AUXILO_EXTRACTING=1`, which tripped the runner's own recursion guard (runner.js main) — every launchd run silently no-opped, so the 2026-04-18 "soak" never actually swept. Applied locally: installer run, agent bootout/bootstrap'd, `launchctl kickstart` cycle executed — 28 sessions discovered, learnings published to auxilo.io, `sweeper.err.log` clean (0 bytes).
> - P1-13a: Same TCC block on the two remaining `~/Documents` LaunchAgents — ✅ DONE (2026-06-10). `io.auxilo.digest` (label since purged of its legacy prefix): daily-digest is a purely local job (summarizes `~/.auxilo/extract.log`), so it got the P1-13 treatment — new `node scripts/runner.js --install-digest` copies `jobs/daily-digest.js` (self-contained, no repo-local requires) to `~/.auxilo/bin/jobs/` and rewrites the plist; agent reloaded, `launchctl kickstart` verified (stderr clean, digest rendered, exit 0). the retraction-sweeper LaunchAgent: **retired as obsolete** — `jobs/retraction-sunset.js` mutates the repo-local `data/learnings.json`, but the production catalog has lived on the Fly volume (`fly.toml` `[[mounts]]`) since cutover, so the local sweep operated on a dead copy. Booted out, plist deleted. No production gap: server.js enforces the retraction window by timestamp at retract-time (DELETE /learn/:id), and nothing reads `retraction_window_active` for behavior — the stale-true flag on Fly is cosmetic. If flag hygiene matters later, port the sunset sweep into server.js's in-process cron. Bloated logs cleaned (~1MB `retraction-sunset.stderr.log` removed with the job; digest logs truncated).
> - P1-13b: daily-digest always reports zero activity — parser/format mismatch — ✅ **FIXED 2026-07-19** (`wave3-client-funnel`, SPEC3 slice A2). Runner now emits `published=`/`held=`/`rejected=`/`account=` tokens on the per-run log lines ONLY (the inner postExtract line lost its tokens — it double-counted), and classifies /learn responses truthfully (2xx `pending_review` = held, not published — every hook item pends today, so old `published=` counts were lies). Digest parses `held=`, aggregates per-builder, and (with credentials.json) renders queue truth as the three SPEC3 lanes: Ready to publish / Needs a score / Needs your eyes + oldest-age + dashboard link; the word "clean" never renders. Operator action after 0.9.3 install: re-run `--install-sweeper` and `--install-digest` to refresh the ~/.auxilo/bin copies.
